How Pardalis Was Hatched

Every skill, decision, generation job, repair, and validation step

Build snapshot. This record documents the actual work completed through approval of all nine standard animation rows and the pet-specific look-mechanics plan. The four cardinal anchors, two 8-frame look rows, final 8x11 assembly, blind direction review, despill, validation, and installation were defined but still pending when this snapshot was taken.

1. Skills and execution capabilities used

Hatch Pet skill

The hatch-pet skill was the governing production contract. It converted the character brief into a Codex-compatible v2 pet, established the 8x11 atlas layout, required all nine standard animation states plus 16 clockwise look directions, and defined every deterministic QA and packaging gate.

Image Generation skill

The imagegen skill was the only visual generation layer. Its default built-in mode generated the canonical base render and every original row strip on a removable chroma-key background. No direct Image API runner, custom image script, or CLI fallback was used.

Bundled workspace runtime

The workspace dependency loader supplied the exact bundled Python runtime and Pillow-enabled environment used by the hatch scripts. A bare system Python interpreter was deliberately not used.

Lightweight visual workers

Each visual job ran in an isolated lightweight worker. Workers generated and visually checked one base image or one row, then returned only the selected source path and a short QA note. The parent process retained control of copying, manifests, deterministic extraction, repair decisions, atlas assembly, and packaging.

Deterministic hatch scripts

prepare_pet_run.py - created the request, prompts, layout guides, and job graph.

extract_strip_frames.py - recovered individual animation frames from each generated strip.

inspect_frames.py - checked frame counts, coverage, components, clipping, and chroma adjacency.

compose_atlas.py - assembled the approved rows 0-8 into the intermediate atlas.

make_contact_sheet.py - produced a row-by-row visual review sheet.

render_animation_previews.py - rendered one looping GIF per standard state.

The required v2 stage also specifies cardinal extraction, look-row registration, extended assembly, despill, atlas validation, direction QA, continuity measurement, and blind-verdict scripts.

2. Source brief and identity translation

The source was a detailed adult male Panther Chameleon (Furcifer pardalis) 3D asset blueprint. It specified the animal's laterally compressed body, elevated cranial casque, rostral projection, turret eyes, zygodactyl feet, curved claws, dorsal and gular crests, heterogeneous scale fields, coiled prehensile tail, and layered PBR skin response.

Identity cues preserved

Hyper-real, production-quality 3D sculpt and cinematic PBR lookdev.

Iridescent turquoise, cobalt blue, and electric yellow structural color.

Deep maroon vertical barring and a broken chalk-white lateral stripe.

Waxy keratin micro-specular highlights with subtle subsurface scattering.

Biologically readable turret eyes, grasping feet, casque, serrated crest, and coiled tail.

Pet-safe adaptation

The original production-asset request was adapted for a 192x208 sprite cell. Micro-detail was simplified only enough to remain legible at pet size. The body became a compact, connected full-body silhouette. The requested tree-bark perch was removed because scenery and detached props would complicate frame extraction and transparency; the feet still pose as if gripping.


3. Run preparation

Infer the pet identity. The name Pardalis was selected from the species name. The description was condensed into a single stable identity sentence for manifests and packaging.

Skip brand discovery. Brand research was unnecessary because the user supplied a concrete animal, anatomy, palette, materials, and desired rendering style.

Select the style contract. The style preset remained auto, with explicit notes requiring an ultra-real cinematic 3D sprite, clean form readability, no floor, no scenery, and no cast shadow.

Select the chroma key. Pure magenta #FF00FF was chosen because the chameleon uses green-adjacent turquoise, blue, and yellow colors. Green or blue keying would risk removing real character pixels.

Prepare the run folder. prepare_pet_run.py created work\pardalis-run, pet_request.json, imagegen-jobs.json, state prompts, retry prompts, and exact layout guides.

Lock the atlas contract. The target was fixed at 8 columns x 11 rows, 192x208 pixels per cell, and 1536x2288 pixels overall with spriteVersionNumber 2.

Generated job graph

The manifest defined up to 13 visual jobs: one base pet, nine standard row strips, one four-pose cardinal strip, and two coherent eight-pose look rows. Dependencies prevented later jobs from starting before their identity and QA inputs existed.

4. Canonical base generation

Read the generated base-pet prompt as the authoritative visual specification.

Generate one centered full-body chameleon on a perfectly flat #FF00FF background.

Require generous padding, crisp edges, no text, no scenery, no shadows, no glow, and no detached effects.

Visually confirm one complete pet with the intended casque, eyes, crest, feet, tail, color bars, and white stripe.

Copy the selected output into decoded\base.png.

Copy the same file to references\canonical-base.png so every row shares one identity source of truth.

Mark the base job complete only after both copies exist.

5. Standard animation rows

Every row was generated from its state prompt, its matching frame-layout guide, and the canonical base reference. Each source strip kept one complete chameleon per invisible slot on flat magenta.

Row 0 - idle (6 frames)

Motion: Subtle breathing, blink, and tiny head/body variation. No large gesture or task behavior. Outcome: Passed.

Row 1 - running-right (8 frames)

Motion: Right-facing drag movement with alternating gait and no speed lines, dust, or floor shadow. Outcome: Required one complete-row regeneration; then passed.

Row 2 - running-left (8 frames)

Motion: Left-facing drag movement with alternating gait. Outcome: Generated independently instead of mirrored; passed.

Row 3 - waving (4 frames)

Motion: Greeting through a forelimb pose only. No wave arcs, sparkles, or symbols. Outcome: Passed.

Row 4 - jumping (5 frames)

Motion: Low-rise, peak, descent, and settle shown only through body height. Outcome: Passed.

Row 5 - failed (8 frames)

Motion: Slumped blocked/cancelled reaction through posture and expression. No red X or detached effects. Outcome: Structurally complete; chroma-adjacent pixels reserved for final despill.

Row 6 - waiting (6 frames)

Motion: Expectant asking motion through coordinated turret eyes, head, and forelimb posture. Outcome: Passed.

Row 7 - running/processing (6 frames)

Motion: Active task work through scanning eyes, focused head turns, and subtle torso/forelimb changes; not literal running. Outcome: Passed.

Row 8 - review (6 frames)

Motion: Slow focused inspection through turret-eye scan, head tilt, lean, and restrained forelimb movement. Outcome: Passed.

Why running-left was not mirrored

The hatch skill allows framewise mirroring only when markings, props, lighting, and direction semantics remain correct. Pardalis has complex biological scale patterns and directional 3D lighting, so a fresh grounded left-facing strip was safer than a mirror derivation.


6. Incremental extraction and QA

Immediately after each strip was copied into decoded, the pipeline extracted only that row and inspected it before marking the job complete. This prevented a bad strip from reaching final assembly and allowed generation of independent rows to continue in parallel.

Checks applied to every row

Expected frame count equals actual frame count.

Every used frame contains a meaningful non-transparent sprite.

The pet is complete, separated from neighbors, and not clipped by slot or canvas edges.

Component extraction produces one coherent pet rather than guide fragments or detached effects.

Identity, proportions, scale, palette, materials, and silhouette remain consistent.

State semantics match the Codex app contract.

Chroma adjacency is recorded but final chroma cleanup is deferred to the one required atlas-wide despill pass.

Running-right repair history

The first generated strip looked like eight poses to the visual worker.

Component extraction found frame slot 03 effectively empty, with only 3 non-transparent pixels.

The approved stable-slots correction was tried to rule out a component-grouping mistake.

Stable-slots still found only 12 pixels in slot 03, proving the source row itself was missing a usable pose.

The complete running-right row was regenerated with the retry prompt and an explicit requirement that zero-based slot 03 be populated.

The replacement extracted all eight poses cleanly with no deterministic errors or warnings.

Failed-row chroma note

Frames 03 and 05 triggered chroma-adjacent pixel counts of 801 and 832. They were not regenerated because the hatch contract explicitly assigns antialiased chroma cleanup to one final deterministic despill pass after the full 8x11 atlas exists.

7. Intermediate atlas and motion review

Extract all approved standard strips into the canonical frames directory.

Run inspect_frames.py across rows 0-8.

Compose final\spritesheet.png and final\spritesheet.webp as the intermediate 8x9 atlas.

Create qa\contact-sheet.png for row-by-row visual comparison.

Render nine looping GIFs under qa\previews.

Send the contact sheet, GIFs, and review JSON to an independent lightweight visual reviewer.

Independent standard-row verdict: PASS. All nine rows preserved the same ultra-real Panther Chameleon identity and 3D style, with complete unclipped frames, clear state motion, correct travel direction, alternating gaits, and no forbidden effects. The two failed-row chroma findings remained deferred.

8. Look-direction mechanics plan

Before generating the 16 direction cells, a pet-specific mechanics document was written. Pardalis must look naturally rather than rotating the entire sprite.

The zygodactyl feet, lower torso, and coiled tail remain anchored to one baseline.

Both turret eyes lead and converge on the target as complete scaly eye structures.

The casque, rostral projection, head, neck, and upper torso follow with restrained pitch or yaw.

Each 22.5-degree step receives a similar motion budget so adjacent poses form one continuous loop.

No whole-sprite rotation, cell tilt, affine warp, replacement eyes, or independently patched look cell is allowed.

Four cardinal pose families

000 up: Pupil apertures above turret centers; snout and casque pitch upward; throat lengthens slightly.

090 screen-right: Pupils and nose cross to the image-right side of head center; head yaws right; far eye and cheek occlude appropriately.

180 down: Pupils below turret centers; snout and casque pitch downward; throat compresses; more casque top is visible.

270 screen-left: Pupils and nose cross to the image-left side; head yaws left; far eye and cheek occlude appropriately.


9. Mandatory completion workflow

Status notice. The following steps are part of the hatch-pet contract but had not yet executed in the captured run. They are included so this record contains the complete production path.

Generate the four-cardinal strip. Attach the cardinal layout guide, canonical base, and approved standard contact sheet. Generate exactly 000 up, 090 screen-right, 180 down, and 270 screen-left in that order.

Extract and approve the four anchors. Use extract_cardinal_anchors.py and compose_cardinal_anchor_strip.py. Any wrong or ambiguous cardinal is a hard failure and must be regenerated before look rows.

Generate look row 9. Create one coherent 8-pose strip in the order 000, 022.5, 045, 067.5, 090, 112.5, 135, 157.5. Register all poses with one shared scale, baseline, and lower-body anchor.

Validate row 9 immediately. Run extended-atlas registration, edge checks, per-direction semantics, and adjacent continuity review. Mark row 9 complete only when no hard failure remains.

Generate look row 10. Use the approved cardinals and completed row 9 as continuity evidence. Create 180, 202.5, 225, 247.5, 270, 292.5, 315, and 337.5 as one coherent strip.

Assemble the extended atlas. Reuse the approved registered row 9 transform, normalize row 10 against it, and create the 1536x2288 PNG/WebP plus registration manifest.

Run the single despill pass. despill_chroma_edges.py cleans all 11 rows together while preserving alpha and reports the authoritative chroma result.

Validate the v2 atlas. validate_atlas.py --require-v2 must confirm exact dimensions, cell population, transparent unused cells, and no blocking chroma contamination.

Create final QA artifacts. Produce the extended contact sheet, focused neutral-plus-16-direction sheet, randomized blind A/B direction sheet, continuity JSON, and motion previews.

Run three isolated blind direction reviews. Each reviewer sees only the unlabeled A/B sheet. Combine verdicts by strict per-cell majority and validate them against the hidden answer key. Cardinal ambiguity cannot be overridden.

Run final independent visual QA. Inspect all 11 rows, every preview, all 16 labeled directions, blind results, continuity metrics, identity, state semantics, clipping, effects, scale, and registration.

Package the pet. Stage spritesheet.webp and pet.json together under the custom pet directory. pet.json must contain spriteVersionNumber: 2 and point to the packaged spritesheet.

Write the run summary and clean intermediates. Keep the final atlas, validation, despill report, contact sheets, direction evidence, previews, review JSON, and run summary; remove disposable prompts and generation intermediates only after successful packaging.

10. Guardrails followed

Never ask the image generator to create or repair a complete 8x11 atlas.

Never synthesize missing rows with locally drawn, tiled, transformed, or code-generated substitutes.

Never patch a new one-off look cell into a coherent generated row.

Never mark a job complete before the selected visual exists in its decoded destination.

Never use detached motion marks, dust, floor shadows, glows, UI, text, guide marks, or scenery.

Never use literal foot-running for the non-directional running/processing state.

Never let the parent process self-approve a repaired look direction.

Never treat intermediate blind uncertainty as equivalent to a wrong cardinal; apply the defined severity policy.

Never run more than the one authoritative final chroma-despill pass.

11. Acceptance criteria for a finished Pardalis package

Final PNG or WebP is exactly 1536x2288 using 192x208 cells.

Rows 0-8 contain the required standard animation frame counts.

Rows 9-10 contain all 16 clockwise look directions in the fixed order.

Cardinal anchors are unmistakable and both look rows form a cohesive continuous loop.

Used cells are non-empty; unused cells are fully transparent.

The extended despill report has ok: true.

validate_atlas.py --require-v2 passes with the run's #FF00FF chroma key.

The contact sheets, row previews, direction semantics, blind review, and continuity evidence have been inspected.

No unresolved identity drift, clipping, scale pop, registration jump, wrong direction, forbidden effect, or broken state motion remains.

pet.json and spritesheet.webp are staged together and pet.json declares spriteVersionNumber 2.

12. Files produced in the captured build

Request: work\pardalis-run\pet_request.json

Job graph: work\pardalis-run\imagegen-jobs.json

Canonical identity: work\pardalis-run\references\canonical-base.png

Generated standard strips: work\pardalis-run\decoded\{state}.png

Extracted frames: work\pardalis-run\frames\{state}\NN.png

Intermediate atlas: work\pardalis-run\final\spritesheet.webp

Standard contact sheet: work\pardalis-run\qa\contact-sheet.png

Animation previews: work\pardalis-run\qa\previews\*.gif

Frame review: work\pardalis-run\qa\review.json

Look mechanics: work\pardalis-run\qa\look-mechanics.md

13. Deliberately unused paths

Brand discovery was skipped because the brief already supplied a complete concrete avatar.

The CLI/API image-generation fallback was not used because the built-in generator succeeded.

True native transparency was not requested; the pet workflow uses chroma-key generation plus deterministic final cleanup.

Running-left mirroring was rejected in favor of an independent grounded generation.

No full-atlas image generation, manual frame painting, or procedural sprite substitution was used.

---
name: hatch-pet
